# Session Log — 2026-10

Append-only. Newest entry at the bottom. One entry per session, written by
the chronicler BEFORE the session's final commit so the entry rides in it.
Format: `## <date> — <issue/trigger> — <outcome>`, then what was
attempted/shipped (commit hash), ratchet movement, decisions, surprises,
friction, next.

## 2026-10-01 — backlog — **the forty-second stamp. The window `2c1c371..fbf1dbf` landed all ten rows of the forty-first band (#1973, #1904, #1876, #1969, #1959, #1963, #1951, #1954, #1942, #1946). `instance` 25556 → 25639 (+83), `schema` 15245 → 15262 (+17), `datatypes` unchanged: 100 flips, zero regressed. New north stars: #1911 (M5, the lax-unresolved ruling; `introspection` 32 measured, `Open` 36 as a hypothesis) and #2006 (M4, filed this pass: XML 1.1-labelled input, `schema` 9 and `instance` 22). Nothing unblocked. Personas not re-run.**

- **Mechanics: `/backlog`, doc-only, on `meta/backlog-2026-10-01`**, surveyed on `main` at **`fbf1dbf`**. A maintenance pass: it claims no issue and holds no `wip/` lease. It replaces `docs/PLAN.md`'s `## Status` section wholesale and starts this file. No code changed. The cartographer wrote this entry itself, because a cartographer subagent cannot spawn the chronicler.

## 2026-10-01 — #1911 — **landed: the lax-unresolved refusal is lifted and the lane's "valid" is ruled key-deep-valid-doc. `instance` 25639 → 25771 (+132), banked in `bc74972`; the issue's +32 bound was stale. M5's north-star hypothesis held: all 36 `Open` cases flipped with the 32 `introspection` ones.**

- Shipped on `wip/issue-1911`: `194fbb7` (`subtreeGate.resolvedChild` admits a lax Wildcard's or lax `{open content}`'s child that resolves no declaration through the new `laxlyAssessed`, which refuses xsi:nil and walks the subtree against xs:anyType; `subtreeGate.enter` factors out the scope push), `f5b9bbf` (instance.go's "Charges at depth", the key-sva clause-1 bullet and two adjacent prose sites restated to the ruling), `bc74972` (the arbiter's bank). Ratchet: **`instance` +132** (pass 132 cases banked fail; 0 regressed, 0 vanished; no other lane moved). `go tool lanestatus` at `bc74972`: `instance` 25771 / 590 / 26361, `schema` 15262 / 136 / 15398, `datatypes` 1163 / 10 / 1173. Surface unchanged.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 1 mason, 2 arbiter**, with no repair and no reject. The arbiter's grounding pass is the expensive one: it ran a probe of both lift variants, and that probe gave the exact +132 the verdict later banked, family by family, with declines 689 → 557. The warden did not run, because the ruling added no validate-side signal and the surface stayed unchanged.
- Thread: GROUNDING issuecomment-5922868608, RULING issuecomment-5922868788, mason's account issuecomment-5923064090, VERDICT accept issuecomment-5923230489.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **The refusal is lifted** (RULING 1). A child of a lax wildcard that resolves no declaration is laxly assessed (cvc-assess-elt clause 3, key-lva). Its [validity] is notKnown, and it is not a charge under either §2.5 definition. A child carrying xsi:type stays refused under GAP(conformance), with #1978 owning the strict arm.
  - **The lane's "valid" is key-deep-valid-doc** (RULING 2). The ruling rests on §2.5's Note, which says the deep-valid clauses exist to catch invalid descendants of laxly validated elements. A charge at or below a lax ancestor therefore still decides "not valid". As a result `decidedNotValid` gains no lax-location decline, and the Acceptance bullet asking for one became moot. That also kept the change out of `xsderr.Error`/`Result`.
  - **The faithful variant over the skip variant.** Both measured +132. Skip reads nothing below the lax child, so it would admit xsi:nil, xsi:type or a fixed `{value constraint}` that the walk does not decide. The arbiter reproduced this: the skip variant fails three refusal rows.
  - **Mason absorbed two stale-prose sites beyond the ruling's list**: the "decided against nothing" shape list and the cvc-id bullet's attribution. The arbiter judged both on merit. The load-bearing row of `TestInstanceExecutorDeclinesUndecidableShapes` became the xsi:type shape.
- Surprises:
  - **The +32 Acceptance bound undercounted by 100.** The arbiter's probe found 132. Seven of those cases lie outside the `any` lexical census: four reach xs:anyType's own lax wildcard, plus open041.v1, wild012.v2 and xsd002.v01. The census bounded the candidates (167) but was not a prediction of flips, as #1642 says.
  - Re-deriving with #1823's and #1891's lax charge arms folded out moved nothing: no banked pass rests on a charge at or below a laxly assessed element. Both rulings would have banked the same cases today.
  - The two new deep-valid rows in `TestInstanceExecutorChargesWildcardChild` pass with the gate change reverted. They pin RULING 2's unchanged behaviour, and the verdict says that is not a cannot-fail finding.
- Friction — **first sighting: the oracle did not read `suite.xml`.** So "which §2.5 validity notion the suite relies on" went unanswered from the corpus. The question was settled from the spec's own Note instead, at no round's cost. The oracle's tools would reach `testdata/xsdtests`, but its standard keeps it to `docs/specs/md/`. Recorded here, not filed.
- Next — discharged before landing: the lift, the prose restatement, the bank and this entry. Owed to the post-land pass: `docs/PLAN.md` lines 73–77, 188 and 227 still describe #1911 as an open ruling and the `Open` cluster as a hypothesis, and M5 needs a new north star. #2008 (a decline names no refusing `subtreeGate` arm) and #1978 (xsi:type under strict particles) now own what remains of the refusals this arm left behind.

## 2026-10-01 — #2006 — **accepted on round 2: a document labelled `1.x` is read as XML 1.0 5e (xml.md §2.8 Note), through the new stdlib-only leaf `internal/xmldecl`, by `parser/xmltree` and both harness raw re-reads. `schema` +9, `instance` +16, banked in `81e2361`. The issue's hypothesis that every case declines on the version label alone was false: 8 of the 31 have other causes.**

- Shipped on `wip/issue-2006` (#2006): `667f84e` (`xmldecl.As10` rewrites the XMLDecl VersionNum to `1.0` at the same length; `xmltree.NewReader` wraps the stream after BOM decoding), `92092bd` (`value/list.go` `listMapping.Parse` splits on #x20 alone, cvc-datatype-valid dv_list §4.1.4 cl.2.2), `bc7eec0` (`TestSuiteAttributeNameAxis` re-pinned at 172,637 occurrences, +72 from the nine `xv00N.xsd` the census now reads), `96e88e5` (repair: `rawDecoder` drops one UTF-8 mark before `As10` in `assessedSubtreeRoot` and `documentVersioned`), `07c774e` (repair: `internal/xmldecl` gets its own ARCHITECTURE `internal/...` entry), `81e2361` (the arbiter's bank). Ratchet, as the verdict states it: **schema +9 (XmlVersions/xv001–xv009 .xsd), instance +16 (xv002.v01; xv005.n01, n02, v01; xv006.n01, n02, n03; xv007.n01, n02, v01; xv008.n01, n02; xv009.n02, n03, v01, v02); zero regressions.** `go tool lanestatus` at `81e2361`: `schema` 15271 / 127 / 15398, `instance` 25787 / 574 / 26361, `datatypes` 1163 / 10 / 1173. `go tool surface -base origin/main`: `surface: unchanged`.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 2 mason (1 repair), 3 arbiter (1 grounding, 1 reject)**. Neither mason round lost work: both were fast-forwarded onto the wip branch whole. The warden did not run, because the surface was unchanged. The arbiter's grounding pass measured in a scratch copy, and that run refuted the issue's hypothesis: 23 of 31 cases flipped, not 31. It also showed that a fix confined to `parser/xmltree` caps `instance` at +9, because the harness re-reads with raw `encoding/xml`. The reject cost one repair round on a defect no corpus case reaches (a UTF-8 BOM before a 1.x label).
- Thread: GROUNDING issuecomment-5923489242, RULING issuecomment-5923489422, MASON issuecomment-5923806066, RESUME issuecomment-5923813133, VERDICT reject issuecomment-5923966963, MASON repair issuecomment-5924178238, RESUME issuecomment-5924184163, VERDICT accept issuecomment-5924387980.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **The accept arm, not the ruling arm** (RULING). The issue offered a ruling arm on the premise that a 1.0 processor rejects a 1.1 label. xml.md §2.8's Note says the opposite: a 1.0 processor processes a `1.x` document as 1.0 and accepts it unless it uses non-1.0 features. So rejecting the label was the deviation. C0 character references and NEL/U+2028 line ends stay unhonoured as non-1.0 features, and no GAP(xml) is owed.
  - **`internal/xmldecl` rather than "inside `parser/xmltree`".** Sharing the rewrite from xmltree would have needed an export (STYLE T5), and a copy in `conformance` would have duplicated a mechanism that must stay byte-identical. ARCHITECTURE's ruling puts an XML 1.0 production two readers share in a stdlib-only `internal/<name>` leaf, so the arbiter ruled the deviation sound.
  - **xv009.n02/n03 were absorbed, not held out.** The label rewrite would have turned them into false accepts through `strings.Fields` splitting on U+0085/U+2028/U+000C. The #x20-only split is local, so the RULING's absorb branch applied, and both are now correctly decided invalid. That makes +16, not the RULING's predicted +14.
  - **Reject finding 1 was repaired in the harness, not in `As10`.** Making `As10` read past a mark would have let xmltree admit a doubled-mark 1.1 document that the harness refuses. Moving `As10` ahead of `decodeBOM` would have dropped UTF-16 1.x admission. `rawDecoder` honours `As10`'s precondition instead, and `TestRawReadsAdmitTheLabelXmltreeAdmits` pins admission set equality in both directions.
  - **The UTF-16 limit was ruled a separate issue.** The raw re-reads decode no UTF-16 document under any label. That was already true for 1.0 labels on `main`, it fails closed, and no corpus case reaches it (93 UTF-16-marked fixtures, none labelled other than 1.0).
- Surprises:
  - Of the 8 cases still failing, two fail on `encoding/xml`'s 4th-edition Name tables (xv001.v01, xv004.v01, U+0133) under either label. Four fail on C0 character references rejected after expansion (xv003.v01, xv006.v01, xv008.v01, xv009.n01). The other two are xv009.n02/n03, the `strings.Fields` defect, which this landing fixed.
  - The arbiter's M4 mutation (As10 removed from both harness decoders) gave `instance` +11, not the grounding's +9 for xmltree only. Five suite-valid cases depend on the harness wiring.
  - The spaced-Eq row in `TestVersion1xIsReadAs10` passes without `As10`: `encoding/xml`'s `procInst` checks only `version=` directly followed by a quote. Mason kept it as a shape row.
- Friction:
  - **The oracle missed `docs/specs/md/xml.md`.** It searched only the schema specs and reported XML 1.1 text absent. The orchestrator quoted xml.md §2.8's Note into the GROUNDING itself, at no round's cost. This is not a first sighting: #979's oracle reported XML's Name productions absent from the local corpus (2026-08-23), and #1668's body claimed XML 1.0 is not in `docs/specs/md/` (2026-09). The documents predict the miss. `.claude/agents/oracle.md`'s description names only "XSD 1.1 / XPath 2.0 / F&O / precisionDecimal", and CLAUDE.md's ground-truth table lists five specs, omitting `xml.md`, `xml-names.md`, `xml-infoset.md` and `xpath-datamodel.md`. This is a finding about those documents, for the post-land pass to file.
  - **First sighting: the session's permission check refused mason's in-place mutation M4** (removing `As10` from the harness decoders), so mason could not measure the harness's share. The arbiter ran it in a scratch copy at no extra round. Recorded, not filed.
- Next — discharged before landing: the label rewrite, the list split, the suiteindex re-pin, both repair findings, the bank and this entry. Owed to the post-land pass (the cartographer, from the accept verdict's ledger):
  1. A tracker: `encoding/xml` uses the 4th-edition Name tables (xv001.v01, xv004.v01).
  2. A tracker: C0 character references are rejected after expansion (xv003.v01, xv006.v01, xv008.v01, xv009.n01).
  3. A tracker: `rawDecoder` reads no UTF-16 document under any label. It fails closed. Closing it means sharing `decodeBOM` and the transcoder through an internal leaf, with its own ratchet attribution over the 93 UTF-16 fixtures.
  4. A candidate for the cartographer or the steward: audit the remaining `strings.Fields` splits over XSD lexicals (`parser/produce*.go`, `parser/conditional.go`, `validate/cvcid.go`, `cmd/goxsd8/validate.go`).

  Also owed: the oracle/`xml.md` scope filing above, and M4's next north star in `docs/PLAN.md`, since #2006 was M4's north star.

## 2026-10-01 — #2013 — **parked on the second arbiter rejection. The change was accepted on round 2 at `e2afd4c` and `instance` +13 was banked at `a935978`, but #2006 landed mid-round, and the orchestrator's merge forward (`09c04fc`) left the rename follow-up unmade: two calls to `documentVersioned`, which the branch had folded into `documentCarries`. The conformance test package did not build. Nothing reached `main`; #2013 is `needs-replan`.**

- Attempted, not shipped. `wip/issue-2013` holds the work, tip `09c04fc`: `2fc92fc` and `816fda5` (round 1: `conformance/instancehints.go` decides a no-schemaTest instanceTest from its root's xsi hints, option 1 narrowed), `64b9df3`, `65844b2` and `e2afd4c` (the repair), `a935978` (the arbiter's bank), `0465a99` (a landing-style LOG entry that this entry replaces; it was never landed), and `09c04fc` (merge of `origin/main` `2d6f222`, #2006). Ratchet on `main`: unchanged by this iteration. `go tool lanestatus` at `2d6f222`, the tree this entry is written on: `instance` 25787 / 574 / 26361, `schema` 15271 / 127 / 15398, `datatypes` 1163 / 10 / 1173. The branch's banked delta, from the round-2 verdict: **`instance` +13 (fail → pass)**, namely MS-Additional addA006, adda007, addA008, addB134, addB155; MS-Particles particlesZ025; MS-Schema schA4; MS-SimpleType stZ059, stZ060, stZ061, stZ063, stZ064, stZ066; 0 regressed. Surface: unchanged.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 2 mason (1 repair), 4 arbiter (2 reject), 1 cartographer, 1 chronicler**. The arbiter count includes the grounding pass and the gate-only round after the merge forward. The cartographer filed #2015 and #2016 between rounds 1 and 2. The orchestrator made the merge forward itself, not in a mason round. The warden did not run, because SURFACE was unchanged at grounding. Rounds 1–3 bought a judged change: the grounding's +13 prediction was met case for case at both full verdicts. Round 4 bought nothing but the park.
- Thread: GROUNDING issuecomment-5923950018, RULING issuecomment-5923953502, MASON issuecomment-5924186570, RESUME issuecomment-5924192264, VERDICT reject (round 1) issuecomment-5924271334, MASON repair issuecomment-5924395758, RESUME issuecomment-5924400603, VERDICT accept (round 2) issuecomment-5924518607, VERDICT reject (gate-only, the second rejection) issuecomment-5924586062, PARKED issuecomment-5924587970. Round-1 finding 4 is noted on #755 (issuecomment-5924518738).
- Decisions made and why:
  - **Option 1, narrowed** (RULING). §4.3.2 clauses 3–5 permit assembling from hints, and declining any shape stays conformant. Clause 5 makes hints global to the assessment, so a root-only reader declines any instance with a hint below the root or an inline `xs:schema`. Without that decline it decides addB139, addB159, addB161, addB162, addB164 and addB166 valid against suite-invalid. The claimed bound is the 38 hinted cases, not the issue's 55.
  - **Passes that rest only on "the hint was followed" are not claimed** (RULING constraint 4). The grounding's root-only probe passed 24 cases, and 11 were discarded by ID: the ordering family addB156–addB169, plus addB063 and addB138.
  - **The mason's two declines beyond the ruling were accepted as absorbed scope** (round-1 VERDICT, ruling (a)). They cover character data after the document element (addB063) and `xmlns:p=""` in a hinted closure (addB138, addB139). The round was rejected on STYLE P3 because neither decline carried a `GAP(` marker or a tracker. #2015 and #2016 now track them, and `65844b2` marks both sites.
  - **Park, not a third round** (round-4 VERDICT; WORKFLOW Parking, PRINCIPLES 30). Two rejections is the hard cap, even though the change itself was accepted.
- Surprises:

- Decisions made and why:
  - **Divergence arm, on three grounds** (RULING). The oracle read §3.3.19 (`xmlschema11-2.md:1546-1547`) as constraining the validation of literals, not declarations: nothing in Part 1 or Part 2 makes a declaration naming `xs:NOTATION` a schema error, and a union keeps a usable member through Datatype Valid 2.2/2.3. Its confidence was medium, because the spec never says when a declaration "uses" NOTATION. The suite contradicts itself: `MS-Particles2006-07-15/particlesZ007` declares `<xsd:union memberTypes="xsd:NOTATION"/>` valid, and simple093 declares the same shape invalid. The adopt arm would regress banked passes, which only a human-filed issue can sanction.
  - **The adopt arm, measured** (arbiter PREDICTION): `schema` +6 −1, `instance` −2. The regressions are `Override/over027/instance/over027.n01.xml` (instance.txt:25061; over027's attribute `type="xs:NOTATION"` is simple091's shape) and particlesZ007 on both lanes (schema.txt:7058, instance.txt:3967). Only simple090, simple092 and simple094 flip without a regression, and the RULING declined to carve them out as unprincipled.
  - **The census:** `suiteindex '*@type|base|itemType|memberTypes={…XMLSchema}NOTATION'` gave 61 occurrences in 61 fixtures. Joined to `schema` it gave 94 entries, 54 banked pass and 6 candidates, identical under `GOXSD_WITHHELD=1`; joined to `instance` it gave 0 candidates and 34 banked pass. `over015a.xsd` was discarded, because no catalog entry names it.
- Surprises: the issue's population held 54 banked passes, not 52 (arbiter correction). The arbiter found particlesZ007, a suite-valid case that contradicts simple093, by joining the census; the oracle did not read the suite and so could not have found it.

## 2026-10-01 — #1899 — **ruled, no code. The 18 `MS-Regex2006-07-15` instance cases are a permanent suite-expectation divergence (PRINCIPLES 25, #1809's form), and their lines stay banked `fail`. The issue's Unicode-version hypothesis was false. `Ratchet: unchanged`.**

- Shipped on `wip/issue-1899`: the landing is this LOG entry only, no code and no tree change, on the claim commit `f736ed6` (base `6e5a545`). The cases are reJ11, reJ13, reJ19, reJ21, reJ23, reJ25, reJ29, reJ31, reJ33, reJ35, reJ61, reJ69, reJ75, reJ77, reS21, reS42, reT63 and reT84. Ratchet: **`instance` unchanged (the 18 lines stay banked `fail`); all other lanes unchanged.** `lanestatus` at `f736ed6`: `instance` 25864 / 497 of 26361, `schema` 15271 / 127 of 15398, `datatypes` 1163 / 10 of 1173. Surface: none.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 1 arbiter**, the two grounding rounds. No mason round, because the ruling changes no code; no warden, because SURFACE was none.
- Thread: GROUNDING + RULING issuecomment-5926719187 (oracle and arbiter blocks, ACCEPTANCE, PREDICTION and the ruling, in one comment).
- Decisions made and why:
  - **No version divergence and no regex defect, for all 18** (arbiter PROBE, a throwaway program). `regex` takes General Categories from Go's `unicode` v15.0.0. All 18 patterns match their whole value and each code point alone, and `goxsd8 validate` exits 0 on all 18. Every value holds a code point above U+FFFF, and its category is the same in Unicode 3.1 as in 15.0, except reT63 (U+1D7CD) and reT84 (U+1D800), which are `Nd` in no version, so `\D` matches them under every version (oracle; §G.4.2.5).
  - **The suite's `invalid` follows UTF-16 code-unit matching**, where each surrogate half is `Cs`. The spec's regex alphabet is code points (Datatypes §G.4.2.2, `Char` covers #x10000–#x10FFFF), so one astral character is one `\p{..}` match. This is the class of `anyURI_a004_1339.i` and #1809. The suite's own metadata (`msMeta/Regex_w3c.xml`) annotates each of the 18 `type='valid'`, and each instanceTest carries a `current` status `queried` (bugzilla 4113, 2007-01-26) over a `prior` status `accepted`. **If bugzilla 4113 leaves `queried`, reopen this ruling.**
  - **The acceptance was restated, not met as written.** Its two-bin sort (version divergence or defect) held none of the 18. Its STYLE P3b form governs a `GAP(` marker, and none may be added on spec-correct code. Its defect clause was vacuous. The ruling lives on the thread and here, as #1809's and #1988's do.
- Surprises: the issue's premise, that the 18 diverge by Unicode version, held for none of them. Two (reT63, reT84) could never have been version divergences, because their code points are `Nd` in no version.
- Friction: none was reported to the chronicler.
- Next — discharged before landing: the RULING and this entry, which is the whole landing. Owed to the post-land pass: close #1899 as the divergence it records, and record the 18 beside #1809's and #1988's in `docs/PLAN.md`'s partition paragraph, so the next `/backlog` excludes them from north-star candidates.

Ratchet: **`instance` +17 (declines 464→447); schema, datatypes and all other lanes unchanged.** The 17 are `ID_IDREF/s3_3_4v{01,02,03,06,07,08,09,10,11,12,22,29}i`, `IdentityConstraint/s2_2_4v{01,02,03}i` and `constraintsOnAttribute/s3_2_3v{01,04}i`. `lanestatus` at `fa3953e`: `instance` 25881 / 480 of 26361, `schema` 15271 / 127 of 15398, `datatypes` 1163 / 10 of 1173. Surface: none (`go tool surface -base origin/main` `+0 -3`, all three methods of removed unexported xmltree types).

- Decisions made and why:
  - **The leaf carries the agreement, not only `Decode`.** The grounding found the body's premise incomplete: a document declaring `encoding="UTF-16"` needs a `CharsetReader`, so a leaf holding only the transcoder would have forced the harness to grow a copy of `agreesWith`/`charsetReader`. `internal/xmlenc` exports `Mark`, `Decode`, `Mark.AgreesWith`, `Mark.CharsetReader` and `Mark.String`, each with a consumer in the module; the mark constants stay unexported, so a `Mark` comes only from `Decode`.
  - **A new leaf, not an extension of `internal/xmldecl`**, so that xmldecl keeps its single §2.8 concern and #361 has the leaf it would extend. ARCHITECTURE gained the internal-leaves entry and the "carry code today" line, as `internal/xmldecl` did.
  - **`instanceHints` now reads through `rawDecoder`**, the grounding's optional named absorption. It moved no case (0 UTF-16 and 0 1.1-labelled documents among no-schemaTest groups) and is pinned by mason's mutation M3.
  - **The UTF-16-mark-under-`encoding="UTF-8"` divergence is documented, not a finding.** xmltree rejects such a document and `rawDecoder` reads it, because encoding/xml never consults a `CharsetReader` for UTF-8. The arbiter reproduced it and checked every caller: `assessedSubtreeRoot` and `closurePrefixUndeclared` walk only documents xmltree built, and an instance xmltree rejects is declined by `assessInstance`. No verdict consumes the permissive read.
  - The four discarded candidates stayed discarded for the grounded reasons: `s3_3_4v04i`/`v05i` (DOCTYPE, `rootStart` declines it), `d4_3_15ii29i`/`ii30i` (suite-invalid, decided before the raw re-read); `s3_4_2_4si03s` has no schema-lane caller of the raw reads.
- Surprises: none. The prediction (+17, from a scratch patch at grounding) met the bank case for case.
- Friction: none reported. Mason's mutation M5 (swapping `Mark.String`'s BE/LE strings) fails no test; that was already true of the moved xmltree code, so it is a test gap carried over, not one introduced, and it is recorded here only.

Owed to the post-land pass: `conformance/schema.go` (step 1 near line 112, about line 339, and `rootReadable` about line 662) still says xmltree's UTF-16 decoding is "not yet implemented", stale before this issue and seen, not touched, by mason; #361/#363 now extend `internal/xmlenc` rather than xmltree, so their bodies want that pointer.

- Decisions made and why:
  - **None arm: no XSD 1.1 rule charges the attribute** (oracle). cvc-complex-type 2.2 needs only a present {attribute wildcard} and cvc-wildcard validity, which has no {process contents} clause. key-governing-ad 3 makes "no governing attribute declaration" a defined state; cvc-assess-attr 1 then does not apply, and cvc-assess-elt 2.2 leaves the attribute not assessed, `notKnown`. The §3.10.1 sentence "strict: There must be a top-level declaration…" (`xmlschema11-1.md:2822`) is descriptive prose with no rule ID. e-validity 1.1.3 names a ·wildcard particle·, and key-att-to attributes an attribute to the {attribute wildcard} itself, never to a particle, so that clause cannot reach it. §5.2 adds that input which is not schema-valid need not be rejected.
  - **The charge arm, measured and declined** (arbiter PREDICTION: a probe in `walk.wildcardAttribute` plus the `subtreeGate.wildcardAttribute` lift, over the full `instance` lane, then reverted): `instance` +7, the 7 IDs, none regressed, declines 447→438. `attgD034.v` and `ctL021.v`, both suite-valid with their attributes declared only in a document named by `xsi:schemaLocation`, would become decided disagreements and stay banked `fail`. The spec wins over the suite (PRINCIPLES 25), so the +7 is not taken.
  - **The acceptance's none clause is met as written**: the ruling names :2822's status and all 7 cases, and no expectation line changes. The charge clause is moot.

  - **The primitive set is generated, not hand-typed or enumerated through `Backend`** (warden pre-flight). A `Backend` method would break every user backend, and `value` cannot import `builtin`. The generator commit precedes the logic commit.
  - **Per member, the existing `(same, decided)` pair, folded SAME > undecided > NOT-same.** An `*xsderr.Error` from Parse is decided non-admission; any other fault, an unmapped primitive, values with neither `Identical` nor `Eq`, and QName/NOTATION without both contexts are undecided. Parse runs on the raw literal, not through `ValidateLexical`, which would collapse whitespace.

  - **Revise → #2045** (`kind/gap`, `area/value`). A user `Override` that narrows a primitive with no redundant member (boolean, the dateTime family, hexBinary, QName) turns this branch fail-CLOSED: Parse's `*xsderr.Error` cannot tell "outside the lexical space" from "in the space but not representable". The fix changes an exported contract across backends, so it is its own issue. Strict narrows nothing, so it cannot fire under the shipped backend.

Part 4 counts: schema 15398 cases, 15 declined, 11 indeterminate; instance 26361, 423, 5 — byte-identical to the grounding's simulation. `lanestatus` on this tree: `instance` 25905 / 456 of 26361, `schema` 15271 / 127 of 15398, `datatypes` 1163 / 10 of 1173. Surface: `go tool surface -base origin/main` → unchanged.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 1 mason, 2 arbiter (0 reject)**: the oracle and arbiter blocks of the grounding, mason's implementation with no repair round, and the arbiter's verdict. No warden ran: the grounding's SURFACE was none.

- Decisions made and why:
  - **The oracle's open point resolved: complexContent restriction has no `##other` facet position.** `xmlschema11-1.md:4841` references `simpleRestrictionModel` inside **xs:restrictionType** (:4831); **xs:complexRestrictionType** (:4850) restricts it and drops that arm. `s4sComplexRestriction` keeps no facet slot, its doc now says why, and a reject row pins it. The arbiter re-derived the same reading.
  - **`##other` as a field of the existing slot, not a separate slot or a walk special case** (STYLE T4). The facet and the wildcard are arms of one `xs:choice`, so a separate slot would make foreign-among-facets an order fault; a special case would encode the model twice. The arbiter judged `other` a fact of its own, not derivable from `admits` (D3).
  - **Three old-acceptance tests rewritten as rejections**, each justified by Appendix A (no element wildcard on element, attribute, simpleType, keybase, selector, field or local element ref): the foreign rows of `TestProduceS4SChildOrderAccepted`, `TestProduceS4SIdentityConstraintChildOrderAccepted` and `TestProduceRefElementChildAccepted`.
  - **#1877's two `GAP(conformance)` notes extended, not a new decline**: `groupDecidable` and `attributeGroupDecidable` skip foreign children, which the producer now rejects; the skip-site comments name #1877 without the `GAP(` token, so the marker count is unchanged.
  - **The facet-position accept rows are pinned by mutation, not by revert**: the old code accepted them too. Mason's mutations 3 (drop `other: true`) and 4 (`holds` admits ·absent·) fail them; the arbiter reproduced both.
- Surprises: none. The `'*//{*}*'` census found 0 candidates under walked owners, the simulation predicted byte-identical logs, and the gate matched it.
- Friction: none the documents did not predict.

Non-blocking.

Merged with origin/main at `62d9130`.   - **The +2 bar on addB138/addB139 was false, and the body was re-stated to "ratchet unchanged" (the grounding's option (a)).** `conformance/instance.go`'s `execInstanceCase` returns fail on `!decidable || perr != nil`, and `HintAt` turns the now-unreadable hinted schema into a `src-import` error, so `perr` is set. The arbiter measured it end to end in a throwaway worktree: `decidable=true perr=[src-import] ... [xml-wf]`, both cases still fail. Option (b), the executor reading a hinted schema's not-well-formedness as invalid, needs its own grounding and could move `instance` +2, so it goes to a separate issue.

For the 1.0 shape the old decline was unreachable once the parser rejects, so only the new 1.1 hinted-schema row tells the lift apart from a no-op.

Mason could not observe read-arm precedence through the real parser (moving the clause 4 check ahead of the read loop fails no test; one shape probed).

Ratchet, from the VERDICT: **`instance` +14 (Id id017.v01–v02, id018.v01–v03, id019.v01–v02, id020.v01–v03, id021.v01–v02; ID_IDREF s3_3_4v04i, s3_3_4v05i), nothing down**. Lane scores after banking: `datatypes` 1163/10/1173; `instance` 25957/404/26361; `schema` 15275/123/15398. Surface: `go tool surface -base origin/main` unchanged.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 1 mason, 2 arbiter**. The arbiter count is the grounding pass and the accept. No repair round, no reject; the warden did not run, because SURFACE was none at grounding. Gate parts 1–4 exit 0 at both mason (on `4a0606e`) and verdict (on `22030e5`).
- Thread: GROUNDING issuecomment-5936586364, MASON issuecomment-5936868330, CHECKPOINT (after merge forward) issuecomment-5936879665, VERDICT accept issuecomment-5937111388. The claim posted no thread comment.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **The bound is 14, not 12.** The filer's DOCTYPE measurement missed two UTF-16 fixtures, `ibmData/valid/S3_3_4/s3_3_4v04.xml` and `s3_3_4v05.xml` (NOTATION and ENTITY declarations, no ATTLIST; banked fail, suite-valid). `go tool suiteindex` cannot census a DOCTYPE, which is not an element, so the grounding took `rg -l --no-ignore '<!DOCTYPE' testdata/xsdtests` (29 paths) through `casejoin join instance`: 22 catalog entries, 0 withheld, 8 banked pass, 14 candidates. A scratch probe moved exactly those 14, and probed the issue's "only refusing arm" hypothesis true for all of them.
  - **The hint path is not separate.** The issue said `instancehints.go`'s DOCTYPE bullet changes only if grounding extended the lift there; `instanceHints` calls the same `rootStart`, so the lift reaches it, and it is sound there (no external subset and no ATTLIST cannot default `xsi:schemaLocation`). The Acceptance was re-stated at grounding.
  - **The `%` refusal is load-bearing**, not over-caution: a parameter entity whose text is `&#60;!ATTLIST` smuggles an ATTLIST past a literal scan (XML §4.4.2, §4.4.8). A literal `%` in a SystemLiteral is refused with it, which the doc says.
  - **A gate-local text scan, not xmltree's DOCTYPE scanner.** Reusing `hasExternalID`/`subsetScan` would mean an export from `parser/xmltree` (STYLE T5), and that scanner reads no ATTLIST.
  - **The oracle bounded the claim**: the refusals guarantee no defaulted attribute, not that the infoset equals the no-DTD infoset; internal general entities and NDATA entities still change it, and the doc names who decides each.
- Surprises: the undeclared general-entity unit row never reached the gate: `parser/xmltree` rejects `&g;` as `[xml-wf] invalid character entity` even when declared, so mason dropped the row and restated the doc to name xmltree as the refuser.
- Friction:
  - **A byte grep misses the suite's UTF-16 fixtures again, this time for a construct `suiteindex` cannot reach.** `suiteindex` (#1239) answers the UTF-16 blindness #1206 found, but only for elements and attributes; a prolog construct (DOCTYPE) sends the measurer back to a grep, and the filer's missed `s3_3_4v04`/`v05`. Second sighting of the UTF-16 class; it cost no round, because grounding re-measured with `rg`. The post-land pass files it (a `suiteindex` census for prolog constructs, or a named UTF-16-aware fallback).

  - The merge forward onto `82494f1` was clean, and `go build` and `go vet` ran on the merged tree before judging, #2026's lesson applied.

- Decisions made and why:
  - **Chain shape, not a typed sentinel, tells a fault from a limitation.** The grounding's probe found every read failure reaches fetch as xml-wf, so the rule cannot discriminate; but the reader's own charges are `xsderr.New` with `Err == nil`, and every limitation (refused charset, BOM-less UTF-16 `*xml.SyntaxError`, internal-subset entity reference, I/O) wraps a cause. A sentinel in `parser/xmltree` or `xsderr` would be an export (STYLE T5) and need a warden pre-flight.
  - **The unbound-prefix exemption reads message text** (`unboundPrefixCharge`, the opening of reader.go:264, 303 and 356), following `srcResolveClause4`'s precedent rather than exporting a marker. A row pins reader.go:264's wording; 303 and 356 share the prefix but no row pins them.
  - **The repair declines rather than re-reading the DTD.** Mason rejected deciding an unbound prefix when `defaultsNoAttribute` clears the composed document's DOCTYPE: larger, and still unable to settle a DOCTYPE with an ATTLIST that leaves the prefix truly unbound. A genuinely unbound prefix now declines; that hazard is `GAP(parser)` at the site, owned by #2073.
  - **The over-lift guard was re-stated at grounding.** iri-001's mechanism is an `&URI;` general entity in the DOCTYPE internal subset (TypeLibrary-URI-RFC3986.xsd:231), not an encoding refusal, so the issue's encoding row was vacuous as its guard; an internal-subset entity row was added, and mason absorbed the attribute-value shape iri-001 actually has. The ratchet cannot see this over-lift, so both verdicts read iri-001 off `GOXSD_DECLINES=1` as a decline candidate.
  - **`rootReadable`'s root decline was not narrowed**; the issue marked it optional.
  - **The oracle's ruling:** a reference that resolves to a not-well-formed resource is an error (src-include 1, src-import 2, src-redefine 2, src-override 1), so schB4/schE5/schH5 are charges.
- Surprises:
  - **The round-1 reject was a reader limitation charged cause-free.** The arbiter drove `<!ATTLIST xs:schema xmlns:xs CDATA #FIXED …>` through `newSchemaExec`: well-formed, and a non-validating processor must supply the default (XML 1.0 §5.1), but the reader charges `unbound namespace prefix` with `Err == nil`, so the case was decided where it had declined. Mason's own "scrutinize hardest" list named reader.go 246 and 289 as sites a future change could turn into limitations; 264, 303 and 356 were limitations already.
  - Several doc sites still gave "well-formed UTF-16 (BOM FF FE) rejected as invalid UTF-8" as the example limitation, false since xmltree decodes BOM'd UTF-16; they now name the internal-subset entity limitation.
- Friction:
  - **The reader's unapplied internal-subset attribute defaults had no owner.** Mason searched (#1733, #2070, #361 adjacent; #2063 closed) and found none, so the orchestrator filed #2073 mid-session and named it in the marker (`0ad0678`). First sighting of a grounding probe that classed charges by chain shape without asking what a DTD can supply; it cost the repair round, so the post-land pass files it at once (enters at grounding: a probe proposing a "fault vs limitation" discriminator did not enumerate what an internal-subset DTD can supply).
  - The merge forward onto `b33a681` (docs only) was clean; build and vet ran on the merged tree before judging.

- Decisions made and why:
  - **Grounding corrected the bound from 17 to 19.** The issue's last Acceptance sub-bullet held 4 `Wild` declines out of reach. Two of them were wrong. wild062.n3 (xs:time against local xs:string) and wild063.n2 (xs:decimal against local xs:integer) each carry an xsi:type child on the lax `xs:any` whose name matches the local `f`. `execInstanceCase` scores an invalid case off `decidedNotValid`, and `cvc-complex-type` is in `decidableRules`, so the charge decides both. The arbiter re-derived the 8 lift-only flips at `b33a681` with a lift probe under `GOXSD_DECLINES=1`, and read the other 11 charge-dependent cases from their fixtures. The plain run on round 1 then printed exactly those 19.
  - **Arm (a): one export, not a third content-model walk in validate.** Option (b) would have had to copy `inSubstitutionGroupOf`, `gatherImplicitElements` and cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec. Implicit containment (key-impl-cont) is mandatory in the export. Without it, a restriction that implicitly contains a top-level name answers with its base's local type, which is wrong. `checkLocallyDeclaredElementTypes` keeps its documented `explicitOnly` under-approximation through the same walk.
  - **`sameType` stays a deliberate duplicate of xsd's `sameDeclaredType`.** `ValidlySubstitutable(anon, anon, nil)` answers false, and the two predicates carry different operand preconditions. Unifying them would export a predicate whose precondition only one caller meets, taking the surface to +2. The warden ruled them boundary twins, each naming the other.
  - **The RULING departure.** The RULING said the gate does not reuse the export, on the premise that nothing would be left to refuse. Round 1's reject showed that the {open content} arm still needs a refusal. In repair the gate calls `LocallyDeclaredElementType` to refuse an {open content} child with a non-·absent· ldt, and the refusal is marked `GAP(conformance)`. The round-2 arbiter ruled this acceptable, on three grounds: the premise was falsified, re-deriving the ldt in the gate would be a second key-ldt-elem walk (STYLE T4), and the refusal is fail-closed and costs nothing. Declines stayed at 352 plus 5 indeterminate.
  - **The repair fixed both halves, not only the gate.** A gate-only refusal would have hidden the false reject from the suite while `goxsd8 validate` kept issuing it.
  - **The attribute half of clause 5 (key-ldt-att) stays out.** It is unmeasured and has no consumer, so `locallyDeclaredAttributeType` stays unexported.
- Surprises:
  - **The deleted refusal had been guarding a second rule.** Lifting it from the {open content} arm let the walk govern a resolved-name child by its top-level declaration. key-governing-ed clause 4.3 withholds that declaration whenever the ldt is non-·absent·. A lax-interleave document with local `e` xs:date and top-level `e` xs:string went from a decline to a decided false reject. The ratchet could not see this, and the arbiter found it by constructing the shape.

The warden built a counterexample with a {type table} selection plus an overriding xsi:type, blocked by the selected type's {prohibited substitutions}. The code charged it correctly, but the prose had it wrong. The xs:error selection (e-props-correct clause 7.2) is the second corner. Both are now rows.

- Decisions made and why:
  - **The oracle bounded the hazard to resolution-dependent rejections.** All six cases are §A grammar faults (`sd-valid`, §2.4 clause 1; §5.1 bullet 1), none a numbered src-* rule. A skipped producer construct can fabricate a `src-resolve` rejection, but not a grammar fault on a lexically present element outside `<annotation>`.
  - **Mason chose "unruled" plus "no unfollowed" over the Notes' "no src-resolve".** Unruled is strictly narrower: `src-resolve` is always an `*xsderr.Error`, and deciding ruled rejections in out-of-subset closures would need a soundness argument per rule. It costs no measured case, since all six are unruled. Unruled alone would decide #1201's resolver fault, which records `UnfollowedLocationUnresolved`, so the no-unfollowed condition keeps it declined. The ENOTDIR row pins it. The verdict ruled the line sound.
  - **The STYLE P3a direction claim lists every decline trigger by identifier, as skipped or rejected unruled.** Mason verified each trigger with a throwaway parse, and the arbiter checked the list against every `return false` site in the allowlist.
  - **Absorbed beyond the body:** `assembleHints` and the test helper `closureGateIn` call `assemblyDeclined`, so neither restates the gate. `assembleHints` is unchanged in behaviour, because the instance lane declines any `perr`.
  - **The lift widens #1883's route, and a new marker says so.** `baseComponent`'s pendingBase refusal is an unruled limitation, not a grammar fault, so out-of-subset closures now read it as a verdict too. No skipped construct produces it and no suite fixture reaches it. The new `GAP(conformance)` marker in `grammarRejection`'s block cites #1883, and step 4's "single exception" sentence now names both exceptions.

  - **The MASON account called the xml:base parse errors in `parser/document.go` unruled. They are ruled** (`RuleXMLWellFormed`, document.go:120), so `grammarRejection` is false for them. The arbiter corrected this in the verdict. It changed no code and no finding.

One wrapping a cause may be a limit of this reader. `conformance/schema.go` now cites the Contract instead of restating it. The repair also corrected `UnfollowedUnreadable`, `ReadDocument` and `CharData.Loc`.

## 2026-10-02 — #2008 — **accepted round 1, no findings. The only new outcome is a decline where a `ValidatingType` failure hits a same-union-node pair, which withholds a charge and manufactures none. The VERDICT accepted it as spec-required.

- Decisions made and why:

(1) The Acceptance bullet's "each rejecting row fails before the fix" was ruled partly false: `Translate("[^cde-[ag]]")` already emitted `\A(?:[^c-e])\z`, which rejects `c`. The `c` row is therefore a guard, not fail-before, and so is the nested-negated row. The fail-before rows are reject `a`, reject `g` and reject the 422 string. (2) The census finds six fixtures, not five. The sixth is `msData/regex/RegexTest_424.xsd` (`([\p{Ll}-[aeiou]]|[^\w-[\s]])+`), whose `.i` banks `pass` and must not regress. The census was `go tool suiteindex 'pattern@value'`, filtered by `\[\^[^]]*-\[`, then `casejoin join instance` and `casejoin join schema`.
  - **Round 1 rejected** (F&O §7.6.1 flag `i`, rule 2). Materialising the complement before RE2's `(?i)` folds it gives a false accept: `Translate("[^Q-[x]]", FlavorFO, "i")` matched `Q` and `q`, which base `(?i)[^Q]` correctly rejected.
  - **Round 2 rejected** (F&O §7.6.1 rule 4). The repair fold-closes the whole subtraction operand, `\p{..}` and `\w` included, but rule 2 adds case variants to a charRange only. Under FlavorFO with `i`, `^[a-z-[\p{Lu}]]$` now rejects `"a"`, a false reject on the exported `Translate`. Folding a materialised class used to only widen it; folding the operand narrows the result.
  - **Parked per WORKFLOW's Parking cap** on the second rejection. The PARKED comment's suggested replacement scope: land the fix for the XSD flavour and for FlavorFO without `i`, which needs no fold and is what moves the two cases, because XSD patterns never carry flags. Track FlavorFO + `i` negated-group-with-subtraction as its own issue, closing only the operand's Char/charRange contributions.
- Surprises: the issue was scoped as an XSD-pattern fix, yet both rejections came from the F&O flavour with flag `i`. That path is reachable through the same exported `Translate`, and neither the body nor the grounding named it. The 424 pattern now rejects a space it used to accept; that is correct by [76], and no suite instance contains one.
- Friction: **grounding scoped the fix by the flavour that moves the ratchet, not by the exported entry point's full domain.** `regex.Translate` takes a flavour and flags, and the FlavorFO + `i` path cost both rejections and the park. This is a first sighting, and it cost rounds, so per this log's rule the post-land pass files it now and does not wait for a second. The partly false Acceptance bullet was caught and restated at grounding (develop step 3), which is the process working.

Ledger items from the round-2 verdict, not findings against this diff, for the cartographer to file or fold: (a) **the rule-4 deviation on the P side and with no subtraction predates this diff**. RE2's `(?i)` folds `\p{Lu}`, `\w` and negated escapes, so `\p{Lu}` with `i` matches `"a"`, and `[^\p{Lu}-[0]]` with `i` rejects `"a"`, at both `origin/main` and the branch. (b) **The fold relation is `unicode.SimpleFold`'s orbit, not F&O's lower/upper-case test** (xpath-functions.md, the flag `i` definition). RE2's `(?i)` already applies that same relation to every class. Owed to the post-land pass: file the grounding-scope friction above.

## 2026-10-03 — #1856 — **accepted round 1 after the replan, no findings. A negated character group with a subtraction now complements before subtracting, so `[^P-[C]]` means (complement of P) − C, per Appendix G [76]'s "G-C" sentence and cvc-pattern-valid. This holds for FlavorXSD and for FlavorFO without `i`. FlavorFO with `i` keeps `origin/main`'s emission byte for byte, pinned by a literal-output row and marked `GAP(regex)`, owned by #2148, with #2147 owning the rule-4 deviation. Ratchet `instance` +2: RegexTest_422.i and RegexTest_430.i (#1856).**

- Shipped on `wip/issue-1856`, tip `9f13a24`, over `origin/main` `348d0a8`. The branch carries `80b26c2`, the parked attempt's core fix. The fold repair `56285f7` is reverted as `bd6a924`. Mason added `e674f16`, the gate: `parser.foldCase` is set only in Translate's FlavorFO arm, and `parseClassBody` complements a negated group only `if neg && !p.foldCase`. Mason also added `ef786ed`, which runs every TestNegatedGroupSubtraction row under both an `xsd` and an `fo` subtest and adds TestNegatedGroupSubtractionFoldCaseUnchanged. `3883db2` merged the round in, and `9f13a24` is the arbiter's bank. Five files changed against `origin/main`, +115/−22: `regex/{classparse,doc,regex,regex_test}.go` and `instance.txt`. Ratchet, from the VERDICT: **`instance +2 (RegexTest_422.i, RegexTest_430.i)`**; `RATCHET-STATE: ratchet run, write committed as 9f13a24`. The arbiter wrote exactly those two `fail`→`pass` lines, which equals the grounding's bound of 2. RegexTest_424.i and RegexTest_426.i still bank `pass`. `go tool lanestatus` at `9f13a24` gives `instance` 26070 / 291 of 26361, `schema` 15288 / 110 of 15398 and `datatypes` 1163 / 10 of 1173. Surface: `go tool surface -base origin/main` is unchanged. The arbiter's gate at `3883db2`: parts 1–4 exited 0, part 2 reported 0 issues, and part 4 took 398.7s, with no Regressed or Vanished case.
- Cost — **this round: 1 oracle, 1 mason, 2 arbiter.** The arbiter count is its grounding plus the accept verdict. The warden did not run, because SURFACE was none. **The whole issue: 2 oracle, 3 mason (1 repair), 5 arbiter (2 reject), 1 park, 1 replan.** The parked attempt is in its own entry above, and the replan rode the second `/backlog` pass. That attempt's two rejected verdict rounds each ran part 4 for about 11.5 minutes. Both fell on FlavorFO + `i`, a path this round leaves alone. What this round kept from the parked attempt is the core fix (`80b26c2`) and the +2 it measured. The takeover was at 10:04Z, GROUNDING at 10:07Z, MASON at 10:22Z and the VERDICT at 10:40Z.
- Thread: TAKEOVER issuecomment-5968069556, GROUNDING issuecomment-5968096441, MASON issuecomment-5968232396, RESUME issuecomment-5968238270, VERDICT accept issuecomment-5968378183. Earlier on the thread: PARKED issuecomment-5966158617 and REPLAN issuecomment-5967585753.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **The takeover reverted rather than cherry-picked.** REPLAN said a fresh claim should start from `origin/main` and cherry-pick `80b26c2`. The session instead merged `origin/main` `348d0a8` into the parked branch and reverted `56285f7`, which left `80b26c2`'s fix on top of `main` with no fold machinery. The tree is the same either way. The branch history keeps the parked evidence in line.
  - **The grounding found the "FO + `i` unchanged" bullet false on the inherited tree.** The complement in `80b26c2` was unconditional, so `[^Q-[x]]` under FO+`i` emitted `(?i)[\x{0}-PR-wy-\x{10ffff}]`, where base emits `(?i)[^Q]`. It therefore required the pin row to compare literal Translate output. It also found that the existing rows ran under FlavorXSD only, so the FO-without-flags rows were owed.
  - **The gate is a parser field, not the `(?i)` prefix or a parameter.** Mason rejected keying on the prefix, because that is output-shaped state. A parameter would have changed two signatures to carry a fact that is fixed for the whole parse. `foldCase` cannot be derived from `flavor` (STYLE D3). The arbiter ruled `strings.ContainsRune(flags, 'i')` sound: `foFlags` rejects any character outside i/s/m/x first, and the field is never set under FlavorXSD.
  - **The tests can tell the gate's width apart.** With the gate widened to all of FlavorFO, only the `fo` subtest fails, on 5 rows. With it removed, both pin rows fail. With the complement block disabled, `xsd` and `fo` each fail on the 5 fail-before rows. The arbiter re-ran the mutations.
  - **FO+`i` is byte-identical to `origin/main`, by two independent differentials.** Mason compared 24 patterns × 5 `i`-flagsets, 120 outputs. The arbiter compared 17 patterns × 10 FlavorFO flagsets, 85 outputs with `i`. All were identical, and the differences fell only in no-`i` flagsets and XSD.
  - **Absorbed: `regex/doc.go`.** Its "Character-class handling … is shared between flavors" was no longer true under FO+`i`. It now names the exception and points at the GAP(regex) marker, and the arbiter ruled the edit correct on its merits (STYLE T6).
- Surprises: none against the prediction.
- Friction: none the documents did not predict. The takeover of an EXPIRED lease, and the grounding's correction of a body premise, are WORKFLOW and develop step 3 working. Mason's part 1 ran `-skip '^TestConformance\z'` because the sandbox refused the `$` spelling. ## 2026-10-03 — #2151 — **accepted round 1, no findings. A hint-less, group-schemaless root whose `xsi:type` names a built-in type is now decided against a schema holding only the built-in components, per the oracle's arm (a1): key-governing-type-elem clause 8 (key-itd), cvc-assess-elt clause 1, cvc-type. The undecidable arms stay declined under tokens of their own: addB199.i as `no-hint-xsi-type-unresolved` and attMd001–011.i as `no-hint-unknown-xsi-attribute`. ctZ011_a.v is not taken and now declines `undeclared-root`. Ratchet `instance` +2: addB202a.i and addB202b.i (#2151).**

- Shipped on `wip/issue-2151`, tip `d06a190`. Mason's commit is `1ec6b10`. `origin/main` `7377c61` (docs only) was merged forward as `c9ba1ee`, which is the tree the arbiter judged, and `d06a190` is the arbiter's bank. Seven files changed against `7377c61`, +208/−44: `conformance/{instance,instance_test,instancehints,instancehints_test,subtreeroot}.go`, the new `conformance/testdata/builtins/builtins.xsd` and `instance.txt`. Ratchet, from the VERDICT: **`instance +2 (addB202a.i, addB202b.i)`; no other lane moved, nothing down, no removals.** That equals the grounding's prediction of +2. `go tool lanestatus` at `d06a190` gives `instance` 26072 / 289 of 26361, `schema` 15288 / 110 of 15398 and `datatypes` 1163 / 10 of 1173. Surface: `go tool surface -base origin/main` is unchanged, so the warden did not run. The arbiter's gate at `c9ba1ee`: parts 1–4 exited 0, and part 4 (`-count=1 -timeout 30m -v`) reported only addB202a.i and addB202b.i as improved but not yet banked.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 1 mason, 2 arbiter.** The arbiter count is its grounding plus the accept verdict. There were no repairs and no rejects. GROUNDING was at 11:15Z, MASON at 11:27Z and the VERDICT at 11:48Z.
- Thread: GROUNDING (oracle ruling, arbiter acceptance and prediction) issuecomment-5968630294, MASON issuecomment-5968717243, RESUME issuecomment-5968721321, VERDICT accept issuecomment-5968863124.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **The oracle split the issue's two arms into three.** (a1) An `xsi:type` that resolves to a built-in type is decided. No element declaration exists, so key-itd supplies the governing type, cvc-assess-elt clause 1 strictly assesses the root, and §4.3.2 clause 3 makes the missing hint irrelevant. (a2) An `xsi:type` that resolves to nothing (addB199.i) is undecidable for the element: key-itd clause 3 fails and the root falls to lax assessment, notKnown. cvc-attribute clause 5 charges only the attribute item, which refines #2081's "not charged" to the element level. (b) xsi-namespace attributes outside §3.2.7's four on a lax root (attMd001–011.i) are undecidable, consistent with #2013's ruling. If the suite banks them `invalid`, that is a suite expectation (PRINCIPLES 25).
  - **The built-ins schema is gated, not handed out to every hint-less case.** The arbiter measured that an ungated `<xs:schema/>` lets the executor decide 14 of the 15, but 12 of those rest only on the strict-start charge in `validate/assess.go`, which is not a ruled charge. Ungated, the lane would read +12 for the wrong reason. `builtinsSchema` hands the schema out only when the root's `xsi:type` resolves, through `resolveQName` against the root's own declarations, and `schema.Type` finds the name. Each other exit has one token: `no-hint`, `no-hint-xsi-type-unresolved`, `no-hint-unknown-xsi-attribute` and `builtins-assembly`, the last a harness fault.
  - **The built-ins come from a committed childless document**, `conformance/testdata/builtins/builtins.xsd`, assembled through `assembleCase`, following `testdata/xlink/xlink.xsd`. Mason rejected an in-memory document fed through `loader.Map`, because `closureVersioned` re-opens every report location from disk and answers true for one it cannot open. ctZ011_a.v would then decline as `versioned`, which is the wrong token.
  - **`subtreeGate.resolveQName` became a free function** `resolveQName(scope []xml.Attr, lexical)` so the root's declarations can be read without a gate (STYLE T4, no parallel resolver). `instanceHints` now returns the root's `xml.StartElement`.
  - **ctZ011_a.v not taken.** Admitting it would mean widening `assessedSubtreeRoot`'s undeclared-root gate, which also moves group-schema cases, and this issue has no ratchet attribution for those. The `TestInstanceExecutorDeclinesUndecidableShapes` row is unchanged, and a locally valid `xsd:int` root is pinned to decline `undeclared-root`.
  - **Tests:** `TestInstanceExecutorDeclinesCaseWithNoGroupSchema` is restated as an 8-row table asserting each exact token, and the new `TestInstanceExecutorDecidesBuiltinTypedRoot` pins the decided arm. Mason ran six mutations. Five fail the tests. The sixth, removing the early `!ok` return after `resolveQName`, is an equivalent mutant: an unbound prefix resolves to a no-namespace name that the `Type` lookup misses anyway. The arbiter confirmed that reverting the `caseSchema` arm fails both tests.
  - **Absorbed:** stale prose in the `instancehints.go` header, the `caseSchema`, `instanceHints` and `resolveQName` doc comments, and `instance.go`'s "What an instanceTest asserts" and cvc-assess-elt hazard paragraphs. The refusal table moved `refuseNoHint` under a `builtinsSchema` heading with the three new tokens.
- Surprises: none against the prediction. The `instance` `no-hint` 15 now partitions as 2 decided, 1 `no-hint-xsi-type-unresolved`, 11 `no-hint-unknown-xsi-attribute` and 1 `undeclared-root`.
- Friction: none the documents did not predict. The mason's three "scrutinize hardest" items were left to the verdict, and the verdict raised no finding against them. Those items are the committed fixture plus its relative path, arm (b) testing the root only, and a root carrying both a resolving `xsi:type` and an unknown xsi attribute being handed the schema.

- Decisions made and why:

Under a governing declaration, cvc-elt 3 already fails, and a second charge is an implementation choice rather than a requirement. RULING 2 therefore restricts the new charge to `!g.hasDecl`, and `nilCheck` stays the only charge under a declaration.
  - **Shape.** The new unexported `walk.instanceNilLexical(e, g)` is called right after `instanceTypeResolves` in `walk.attributes`, so it runs on every element. To keep one encoding of the four literals and the unreadable state (STYLE T4), the mason split `instanceNil`'s mapping into `instanceBooleanOf`. The mason rejected `value.ValidateLexical` with `causedBy`, because `nilled` documents its choice not to read xsi:nil through the backend. As a result this is the one cvc-attribute clause 3 charge that wraps no String Valid cause, and `d904485` corrects `validate/doc.go`, which had said every such charge wraps one.
  - **Prediction bound 0.** `suiteindex` over every `xsi:nil` found one non-boolean lexical in the corpus, wild042.n1.xml (`nil="1234"`), and it is already banked `pass`. `casejoin join instance` gave 0 candidates. A `GOXSD_DECLINES=1` run showed no instance decline under either nil token.

Deleting the `hasDecl` guard fails three declaration-side tests, which pins RULING 2.

It was not counted against this landing, because of RULING 2 and that precedent. The mason had flagged it as "scrutinize hardest" item 1.

The walk no longer charges cvc-attribute against the `xsi:nil` or `xsi:type` of an `unattributed` element: `walk.instanceNilLexical` and `walk.instanceTypeResolves` return without charging or declining when `g.unattributed`, because where attribution was declined the true schema may skip the element (key-skipped, key-governing-ad clause 3 and its Note, cvc-assess-elt clause 2). `walk.localGovernance`'s `ValidlySubstitutable` exit, the one `unattributed` shape that is attributed, discloses the withheld charge under a `// GAP(validate):` naming #1892. `conformance/instance.go` case 4 is restated to every cvc-attribute charge the walk makes. Ratchet `instance` unchanged, which equals the prediction (#2159).**

The mason's commit is `a41632f`. `origin/main` `4a6239a` (docs only) was merged forward as `9aa8c42`, and that is the tree the arbiter judged: 6 files, +108/−22, `validate/{assess,cvcattribute,cvcassesselt_test,doc}.go` and `conformance/{instance,subtreeroot}.go`. Ratchet, from the VERDICT: **`unchanged`; `ratchet run, tree clean, nothing to bank.`** `go tool lanestatus` at `9aa8c42` gives `instance` 26078 / 283 of 26361, `schema` 15288 / 110 of 15398 and `datatypes` 1163 / 10 of 1173. The arbiter's gate at `9aa8c42`: parts 1–4 exited 0. `go tool surface -base origin/main` reports unchanged, so the warden did not run.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 1 mason, 2 arbiter.** The arbiter count is its grounding, which measured before and after with an unbanked `TestConformance` run, plus the accept verdict. There were no repairs and no rejects. The mason took about 18 minutes and the judging arbiter about 14. GROUNDING was at 14:18Z, MASON at 14:36Z and the VERDICT at 14:57Z.

The filing at #2061's post-land pass is summarised on #2061, issuecomment-5969431082.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **Skipped is undetermined, not absent.** The oracle ruled that where attribution was declined, ·skipped· is unknown, so a charge asserts an unestablished fact; the decline is repository design that the spec's conditions justify. It kept key-att-to's "not attributed to any component" distinct from ·skipped·.
  - **No new record.** The arbiter ruled the guard records nothing: `walk.child`'s `a == nil` comment already guarantees every nil-attribution parent carries a record or violation, and `walk.attribute` already logs the xsi item ungoverned, so a log line here would duplicate it.
  - **Arm (a), the blanket guard.** `localGovernance`'s `ValidlySubstitutable` error exit also builds `unattributed`, on an element that IS attributed to the {open content} wildcard, so the blanket guard withholds a correct `xsi:nil` clause-3 charge there. The orchestrator chose the blanket guard over telling the shapes apart, because the exit is unreachable for a finalized schema (`xsd.validlyDerived`: src-resolve 1.1 is charged first in Phase A) and a distinguishing field would cut against STYLE D3 and #1892's "unattributed unrepresentable beside hasDecl". The disclosure is a `GAP(validate)` naming #1892, with the direction stated unestablished (STYLE P3a).
  - **Rejected: a guard on `g.typ == nil`.** It would silence the declared-but-declined shape and break `TestDeclarationlessXSINilChargesClauseThree`'s lax row.
  - **Prediction 0.** `suiteindex` over every `xsi:nil` gave 92 occurrences in 75 fixtures and 0 `instance` candidates. Over every `xsi:type` it gave 617 in 333 fixtures and 2 candidates (ST_targetNS00101m2_p, addB199.i), neither able to flip upward, since the guard only removes charges on elements whose Result already carries a record or violation.

Mason ran three mutations and each fails the rows it should.
  - **Absorbed:** the stale-prose sweep in `walk.attributes`' doc, `validate/doc.go`'s xsi paragraph, `instance.go`'s key-sva clause 2 bullet and `subtreeroot.go`'s `nilValue` doc.

The orchestrator kept the wording because the disclosure is correct and the exit unreachable; the rewording rides #1892's next edit of that block.

A per-open-element scope stack confines the decline to hinted elements.

The arbiter ruled the one behaviour change (an instance with a below-root hint now has its root's uncovered hints added, where before all were withheld) inside that ruling: the withheld set only shrinks. `TestGroupSchemaReadsRootHintsAlone` pins both halves.

- Decisions made and why:
  - **Arm: read the inline schema.** The oracle ruled that §4.3.2's "processor and application dependent" admits both arms, that a processor that reads an inline schema treats it as an ordinary schema document (§5.1, §B.3), and that the inline `xs:schema` element itself is laxly assessed (cvc-assess-elt clause 3) and charged nothing. The arbiter chose the reading arm because it decides three suite-valid cases.
  - **Mechanism.** `hintReader` serves each inline `xs:schema` through `ParseSet`/`RootAt` at a synthetic `<instance>#inline-N` location, carrying the namespace bindings inherited from its ancestors (grounding constraint (a)). `closureVersioned` reads the instance itself for an inline location instead of re-reading the synthetic path from disk (constraint (b)). `refuseInlineSchema` is narrowed to a nested inline schema, and the new `refuseInlineUnread` keeps a parser read error from reading as "not valid". The groupSchema path is unchanged in effect (constraint (d)); addB169.1.v stays `pass`.
  - **addB166.i: decided valid against the suite's invalid, status "queried".** No named rule charges it, so it is recorded as a PRINCIPLES 25 divergence in `docs/PLAN.md`'s divergence bullet, beside addB159/161/162/164, and in `instanceHints`' doc. addB142.i (`hint-undeclared-root`) and addB181.i (`schema-error`, src-resolve 1.1) stay banked `fail` on declines.
- Surprises: none beyond the grounding's probe findings, each pinned before the mason round.
- Friction: none this session.
- Next — discharged before landing: the merge forward, the ratchet write and this entry. Owed to the post-land pass: two non-blocking VERDICT findings. **STYLE E3:** `closeSchema` (`conformance/instancehints.go:485`) adds the inherited bindings inside the inline start tag, so a column later on that line is shifted; line numbers stay exact and no verdict reads the column. **STYLE T1:** `isInlineLocation` (`conformance/instancehints.go:577`) matches any location with the `<base>#inline-` prefix, not only the ones `assembleHints` served; that belongs with #755, which could pass the served set in if it re-homes the reader. ## 2026-10-04 — #2187 — **Accepted in round 2 after one reject and one repair. `internal/xmltok` is a fork of Go 1.26.0 `encoding/xml`'s strict tokenizer whose name checks read XML 1.0 5e [4]/[4a] from `internal/xmlname`; it returns `encoding/xml`'s own token and `*xml.SyntaxError` types. The repair added the leaf `internal/xmlchar` (`IsChar`, [2] Char), read by xmltok and by `parser/xmltree`'s `charRef`. No call site moves yet; that is #2188. Ratchet unchanged, surface unchanged (#2187).**

- Shipped on `wip/issue-2187`: `638caba2` (the xmltok fork), `8fdd27e7` (the CharsetReader differential row made to discriminate), `d750ab76` (ARCHITECTURE names xmltok in the internal leaves box), and the repair `e27658b1` (`internal/xmlchar`). Ratchet, from the round-2 VERDICT: **unchanged**, `ratchet run (GOXSD_RATCHET=1), expectations tree clean, nothing to bank`. The grounding's PREDICTION, "instance: unchanged; schema: unchanged", was met. `go tool lanestatus` at `4ddee0ad`: `instance` 26093 / 268 of 26361, `schema` 15288 / 110 of 15398, `datatypes` 1163 / 10 of 1173. `go tool surface -base origin/main` printed unchanged, so no warden round. Arbiter gate at `e27658b1`: parts 1–4 exit 0, part 2 `0 issues`, part 4 with `-timeout 30m -v` and no Regressed, Vanished or improved-but-unbanked case. `go list -deps ./conformance ./parser/... ./cmd/... ./validate/...` reaches no xmltok.
- Cost — **1 oracle, 2 mason (1 repair), 3 arbiter (1 reject).** The arbiter rounds are its grounding share and two judge rounds. Claim `06c6c5ea` 02:05Z, GROUNDING 02:08Z, MASON 02:24Z, reject 02:36Z, repair 02:45Z, accept 03:06Z. The repair round bought one home for [2] Char; the reject named no other finding.
- Thread: GROUNDING (oracle and arbiter) issuecomment-5975650015, MASON issuecomment-5975755914, VERDICT reject issuecomment-5975839974, MASON repair issuecomment-5975893588, VERDICT accept issuecomment-5976025269.
- Decisions made and why:
  - **Fork, not rewrite, and in one session.** The arbiter sized the tokenizer core at 944 non-blank non-comment lines and ruled "do not split". A rewrite would lose the byte-for-byte offset and error parity that the differential pins. Returning `encoding/xml`'s own types keeps #2188's diff at the constructors and lets the differential compare with DeepEqual.
  - **The arbiter measured the acceptance rows at grounding instead of trusting their wording.** On go1.26.0, `<a a&b="0"/>` is not a name-table fault: `readName` stops at `&`, and the error is "attribute name without = in element" at offset 5 (1:6). Because the bullet says "as encoding/xml reports it", that row was pinned to that error and offset, and `<-04-29/>` ("invalid XML name: -04-29", offset 7) carries the 5e location convention. The grounding also set the differential's verdict bars: malformed documents in the corpus, so that identical errors cannot pass vacuously, and 5e rows, so that a re-export cannot pass. The mason's corpus has 65 rejected documents, and each 5e row first asserts that `encoding/xml` rejects it.
  - **A restyled fork, with one disclosed departure.** `.golangci.yml` fails a verbatim fork (17 `else` sites, `err == io.EOF`), so `rawToken` and the directive's `goto HandleB` were split into helpers and a `directiveState.step` loop. The arbiter checked these line by line against go1.26.0. End of input is matched with `errors.Is(err, io.EOF)`, so a wrapped io.EOF reads as end of input. `doc.go` discloses this; the arbiter accepted it, since no #2188 consumer reaches it.
  - **Round-1 reject: duplicate [2] Char (STYLE T4, ARCHITECTURE's one-home rule).** xmltok's `isInCharacterRange` re-encoded the production already inline in xmltree's `charRef`. The repair made the sibling leaf `internal/xmlchar` rather than adding to `internal/xmlname`, because xmlname's doc limits it to the name productions and `regex` has no use for [2]. ARCHITECTURE now states xmltok → xmlname and xmltok → xmlchar as the only internal-on-internal edges, and the arbiter confirmed this over `./...`. A throwaway differential found `IsChar` equal to both deleted encodings on 1,245,188 inputs.
- Surprises: none beyond the `<a a&b>` measurement above. The oracle had predicted a fatal error there too, ruling by question class; only the arbiter's run fixed which error and offset.
- Friction:
  - **The oracle ruled by question class because it has no gh** and could not read the Acceptance bullets. This is #2142's subject (open, `kind/process`), and it cost no round, because the arbiter measured the bullets.
  - **The permission classifier denied a mason's call-site mutation test run.** In the repair round, the mason's probe forcing `charRef` legal and turning xmltok's text check off was refused. The arbiter ran those mutations at the verdict, and all were caught. This is a first sighting, it cost no round, and it is not filed.
- Next — discharged before landing: this entry. Owed to the post-land pass: unblock #2188, which is `blocked` on #2187. #2188 owes the reconciliation the mason left deliberately: ARCHITECTURE's `parser/xmltree` box, `parser/xmltree/doc.go:5`, and retiring the `GAP(xml)` markers at `parser/xmltree/reader.go:91`, `entity.go:178` and `doc.go:37` once xmltree imports xmltok.

- Decisions made and why:
  - **Constructors and declarations swapped, with no wrapper.** The four sites are `Reader.dec`/`NewReader`, `(*content).include`, `subtreeroot.go`'s `recordingDecoder`/`rawDecoder`/`documentEnd`/`rootStart`/`subtreeGate.dec`, and `instancehints.go`'s `hintReader.dec`. The two conformance files are type-coupled, since `recordingDecoder` feeds `hintReader.dec` and `rootStart`, so they moved together. The grounding showed that moving `parser/xmltree` alone leaves xv001.v01 declined (`decode`).
  - **The prediction came from a differential scan, not `suiteindex`.** The grounding ran a scratch differential over all 41,839 suite .xml/.xsd/.dtd/.ent files, each read the way the four sites read it. `encoding/xml` and xmltok differed on exactly xv001.v01.xml and xv004.v01.xml, and xmltok rejected nothing that `encoding/xml` accepts. At `995c6ed`, `suiteindex '{*}Dĳkstra'` returned 0 and listed both files among those it could not read ("invalid XML name: Dĳkstra"), so it was blind exactly where the construct sits.
  - **ARCHITECTURE restates the rule rather than adding an exception.** The `parser/xmltree` box now reads "an internal/ leaf whose own module edges, if any, are internal/ leaves". The VERDICT judged it sound.
  - **The `-04-29` rows guard "as today" and are not evidence for a site.** They pass on both trees. Mutation evidence: reverting `reader.go` fails the document `Dĳkstra` row, and reverting `entity.go` fails the replacement-text row. The conformance sites carry no unit row, and their evidence is the two lane flips.
  - **The mason absorbed three more stale `encoding/xml` attributions** (instance.go's `refuseDecode` prose and ARCHITECTURE's "one `encoding/xml` reader"). It left six `subtreeroot.go` comments alone as parity statements. VERDICT finding 1 rules on those (below).
- Surprises:
  - **The landing cures the `suiteindex` blindness it worked around.** `tools/suiteindex` reads through `parser/xmltree`, so at `8c86119` `go tool suiteindex '{*}Dĳkstra'` prints `2 occurrence(s) of {*}Dĳkstra in 2 fixture(s)`, xv001.v01.xml and xv004.v01.xml. A later census of a 5e-name construct can trust the tool.
  - **The oracle flagged a possible suite quirk, out of scope:** `xv004.xsd` declares `A&#x30000;`, and U+30000 is above #xEFFFF, so it is outside 5e [4]/[4a]. xv004.v01.xml depends only on `Dĳkstra`. The ruling was "do not widen [4]", and nothing is filed.
